Be my guest.]] [[Try it.]] ...so maybe I'll focus on the amendments that we're going to make at second reading, taking your vote further into account, even here in this Chamber, in such a little cultural way. What amendments do we propose? Firstly, in municipalities up to 20,000. leaving one-mandate electoral districts. We believe that with all the disadvantages of this ordination, with all its paradoxes in the smallest municipalities they are relatively less frequent, they are partially balanced by certain advantages. Therefore, in the case of municipalities up to 20,000. We'll report an amendment to make it a one-mandate electoral district. We're reducing the number of commissioners. The State Election Commission had such a point that it could not cope with the appointment of almost 400 electoral commissioners. Okay. We therefore propose that this be one Commissioner per Senate constituency, or 100 Commissioners, about which we are about to discuss. They will also have slightly less work in the forthcoming elections, and this smaller number should deal with this. We even change the content of the first page of the ballot. The state committee, failing to see options such as the change in font size, raised such reservations that it was not possible to accommodate all the information on the front page. Okay, we're not gonna have to change the font size. We're changing the order of the ballot pages. So that on the front page there will be a title and instructions on the correct way to vote. In the forthcoming elections - this is a major change and show that these accusations were absolutely unfounded - we are introducing a transitional provision stating that in the forthcoming elections they will still be local governments, communal councils, district councils will decide what the electoral districts will look like. [[And then? And then?]] Moreover, it is also the answer to these allegations that the PKW will not manage that the electoral system will not cope with a large number of new electoral districts. Well, these new electoral districts are almost gone, because we are also moving away from changing the size of electoral districts in elections, for example. to the Seyms. So that these new electoral districts will actually only be established in municipalities over 20,000. residents where we go to a proportional ordination. So there will be very few new districts. Moreover, the council of the municipalities will decide about them. We are also moving away from a highly criticized solution which was intended to increase the pool of candidates available to voters, i.e. to allow candidates who also live outside the municipality to be elected to the municipal council. Now we are often faced with such a situation that experienced local government officials have to pay their seats because they have just bought an apartment in a neighboring municipality. It happens, there are also known cases, that the secret is that one or the councilman hides his real place of residence just so as not to lose his mandate. We wanted to put an end to this paradox, but since it's such a resistance, there you go, we let go, we leave the domicile requirement in the elections to the commune council. We support the proposal - it was the idea of our coalitionists from the Agreement party - to extend the term of office of local governments to 5 years, especially in the context of introducing a limitation of two-cadence, so that 10 years would be such a time to complete certain projects. We're moving away from a solution that means that it's the uncle who's calling the treasurer. It seemed consistent and logical to us, and it is logical and consistent with the executive system, but once again, since this rule causes such controversy, we do not insist here. This is not a key provision, it does not increase citizens' rights, and this is what we particularly care about. Finally, a change in the composition of the Regional Election Commissions. There have been allegations that it will be difficult to find enough candidates to work on these regional committees in view of the introduction of two regional electoral committees. We introduce the principle that the minimum composition required for the committee to operate is five members, if there were not enough electoral committee reports to create such committees. So with two committees, it's 10 members, which is as much as it is now, because now it's up to 10 members on the regional election commission. [[♪ The bell ♪]] So that the arguments that we're gonna run out of people here, you can give up now. In conclusion, out of the 18 comments made by the State Election Commission, we took 14 into account a lot of opposition, the social side. ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u259	Thank you very much, Member. Please, Mrs Elizabeth Stępień, the Modern Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u260	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the club Moderna vis-à-vis the draft law amending the Act on Insurance and Insurance Activities and some other laws, print No. 2068, and the report of the committee is print No. 2100. The draft amendment of the law adapts Polish law to European Union legislation relating to insurance and reinsurance activities. The bill is of a sequencing nature. It is based on the reasoned opinion of the European Commission addressed to the Republic of Poland on the basis of Article. 258 of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union. The aim of the revision is primarily to define insurance entities. Thus, the provisions of the contested law extend the concepts of the leading company and the financial entity. The proposed solutions also concern the pursuit of activities by internal insurance undertakings and internal reinsurance undertakings, the conclusion by the national insurance undertaking of co-insurance contracts for large risks located in other European Union countries and the conclusion by foreign insurers of co-insurance contracts for large risks in Poland. In addition, the act regulates the possibility for the Financial Supervision Commission to prohibit or restrict the free disposal of assets located in Poland by a foreign insurer or insurer from a Member State of the European Union operating in our country by a branch or on the basis of the freedom to provide services. Under the new provisions, the restriction or prohibition of the free regulation on assets will be made at the request of the supervised State of the European Union in which the establishment is established. The amendment also introduces amendments to the Act on Export Insurance Guaranteed by the State Treasury in respect of the number of members of the corporate board and changes the wording of the Act on Bonds. They are linked, among others. with a minimum nominal value of a subordinated bond of 400 000. zł in the case of its emissions, e.g. by national banks, cooperative credit and savings banks or insurance companies. Transitional provisions are also proposed for transferable securities or other financial instruments based on converted loans which were issued before 1 January 2011, for the calculation by insurers and insurers of the sub-module of share price risk, and for the issuance of subordinated bonds before the entry into force of the Act. Therefore, the Modern supports and does not comment on this proposal. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u261	Thank you very much, Member. Please, Mrs Genowefa Tokarska, the Polish People's Party.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u262	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The changes introduced, i.e. these proposals from the project, result from the incomplete alignment of Polish law with the two directives of the European Union, which the European Commission drew Poland's attention to in May this year. This concerns the implementation of the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council on the taking up and pursuit of the business of insurance and reinsurance - so-called. Solvency II - and Parliament and Council Directives amending Directives and Regulations as regards the powers of European supervisory authorities, including insurance and occupational pension schemes supervisory authorities, as well as exchanges and securities. This Directive is called Omnibus II. The Financial Supervision Commission agreed with the reasoned, legitimate opinion of the European Commission. It considers these changes to be correct. Therefore, the draft law introduces, complements the arrangements that concern the pursuit of the business of captive insurance and reinsurance undertakings and includes definitions and capital requirements, as well as arrangements that lay down rules for the co-insurance of large risks in the European Union, the possibility for the Financial Supervision Commission to prohibit the free disposal of assets located in Poland, as well as transitional measures concerning transferable securities and capital instruments acquired until 1 January 2016. The bill also regulates the composition and functioning of the Management Board of Export Credit Insurance Corporations. According to the government's proposal, the board of directors of the company will consist of no more than four members, which will ensure better, more flexible management. The project also sets the minimum nominal value of the subordinated bond at 400 000. zł, if the issuer is a national bank, SKOK, National Cooperative Savings &amp; Credit Fund, brokerage house, insurance undertaking or reinsurance undertaking. This version of the project The Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party accepts. I do not know how this draft amendment, which is being tabled today by Law and Justice, will affect this. I think we'll come up with a final position after work on the Committee on Public Finances. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

To a large extent, this level came from the fact that... Please note that in the main perception we are talking here about institutions, largely about banks, and banks have deposit protection of up to 100,000. the euro, i.e. under the current conditions is That's about 400,000. zł. If we had introduced this amount saved in euro, it would still be a problem with the fact that this amount would change banks, the denomination of the bonds would not be certain in the sense that from the point of view of the practical buyer of the bonds, as they buy one piece, they would prefer it to be, for example. $400,000, $300,000. If we'd brought in 100,000. The euro, this upper amount would be very mobile, could be as high as possible. 457,000. So then from the point of view of billing and buying bonds for the denominations... An investor needs to buy bonds, for example. for PLN 10 million. So how many of these bond pieces are we talking about? And it's $400,000. It makes things easier to count. That's why she's in gold. That's one thing. Why the sum of 400,000.? Because, like I said, deposit protection is up to 100,000. The euro, so it was established that there was more or less this perception that it was the same amount as for deposits. The implementation of the Bankruptcy Directive introduces the concept of MREL, i.e. capital, which will be, let's call it, used to reduce the risk that taxpayers' money should not be used during the resolution, but to a large extent investors' money will be used, and then capital will be moved, let's call it, MREL-owski, and thus more risky. Now the situation is that subordinated bonds will be part of MREL's capital, while the awareness of individual investors in us is largely such that the bonds are very often associated with something very safe. If it's bonds issued by a bank, it's a perception that it's a deposit. So we wanted to avoid this kind of complication, because the Italian experience showed that there were a lot of subordinated bonds issued by banks that were acquired by individual investors, and then during the orderly bankruptcy of one of the Italian banks, there was a problem because unconscious private investors would lose money because they were convinced that they were buying bonds, i.e. it was like a deposit in banks. This experience shows that it's... In general, the European Commission has begun to consider and will revise this directive. I'm skipping the other inaccuracies there, but this is one of them. We wanted to avoid this situation and already introduce what has been very positive adopted by practically everyone, so to speak, stakeholders of this provision. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u269	Thank you very much. The discussion is closed.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u270	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The project concerns the alignment of Polish law with EU legislation relating to insurance and reinsurance activities, certain provisions of the EU Solvency II and Omnibus II directives on insurance. These directives have already been implemented under the Insurance and reinsurance Act of 11 September 2015 and several regulations, but in its opinion the European Commission pointed out that the different provisions of the two Directives had not been implemented in the national rules on insurance and reinsurance activities. The new solvency rules are based on the idea that insurance undertakings should be able to cover smoothly the losses associated with the fulfilment of the risks they cover from the accumulated capital for this purpose. The new system is therefore intended to increase confidence in the insurer's compliance with its obligations. In addition to many new requirements, responsibilities, extensive substantive and logistical work resulting from the implementation of Solvency II, it also provides benefits. One positive side of the new regulations for insurance companies is to achieve optimal capital allocation by adapting capital requirements to identified risk factors. The main advantage for policyholders lies in a high degree of protection against the risk of bankruptcy by ensuring that the capital of the insurer is at an appropriate level. However, Solvency II also means a significant increase in capital requirements for insurers. Moreover, this calculation will be based on a wider range of issues than it currently is, among others. take into account market and credit risk. Insurance companies also have to meet a number of administrative and organisational requirements and reporting requirements due to the assumptions of the newly designed management system. The bill is worthy of support in view of the necessity of its implementation, which the European Commission imposes on us. It is important that the potential benefits are not covered by the costs that policyholders will have to bear. I also hope that they will not increase costs for customers.

And these shortcomings concern, as the judgment points out, medical equipment, auxiliary equipment, other devices, as well as products which are not usually used to protect health. In other words, they are listed in Annex 3 to the VAT Act. 82, 92 and partly in position. 103. ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u292	Dear Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I have the honour to present a report on the government draft law amending the Environmental Inspection Act and the Environmental Protection Law. The draft Act amending the Environmental Inspection Act and the Environmental Protection Law aims at adapting Polish law to the provisions of Directive 2015/1480 of the European Commission of 28 August 2015 amending certain annexes to Directives 2004/107/EC and 2008/50/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down provisions on reference methods for the approval of data and the location of measuring points to the assessment of air quality. It was the subject of work by the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry on 7 December. It was then at the committee meeting that the first reading of the government's submission took place. The main amendment of the current provisions resulting from the transposition of Directive 2015/1480, is the amendment of the Act of 20 July 1990 on Environmental Protection Inspection, which introduces additional tasks for the National Reference and Patterning Laboratory operating at the Main Environmental Protection Supervisor with its registered office in Krakow. Therefore, the following solutions were adopted in the framework of the transposition: the imposition on the national laboratory of responsibility for the coordination process in Poland of quality assurance programmes organised by the Joint Research Centre of the European Commission, the coordination by the national laboratory of the proper application of reference methodologies and the demonstration of equivalence of non-reference methodologies, the participation of the national laboratory at least once every 3 years in quality assurance programmes organised by the European Commission. In addition to the transposition of Directive 2015/1480, the draft law also provides for changes resulting from the analysis of the current air monitoring system in Poland. These changes are a comprehensive solution with regard to the principles of modelling mathematical transport and the transformation of substances in the air at national level. The solution proposed in the project will provide systematic and scientific support to the Environmental Inspections, i.e. both the Regional Environmental Inspectorates and the Chief Environmental Inspector responsible for conducting country-wide air quality assessments. The proposed amendments to the Environmental Law will concern primarily the establishment of the Institute of Environmental Protection as the body responsible for the implementation of mathematical modelling of transport and the transformation of substances in the air at national level and the development of the scope and use of the results of mathematical modelling referred to above. The law is compatible with European Union law. The deadline for transposition of that Directive expired on 31 December 2016 and attention should therefore be paid to the urgency of the draft law in question. At the same time, it should be added that in order to fully carry out the tasks set out in the draft law and to make use of the planned financial resources, Members proposed an amendment to the article. 3 of the draft law on the transfer of unused funds in 2017 is the amount of 920 000. zł, for 2018 in order to spend them - it is a matter of Article. 3 (1) (a) Point 2. In the face of these changes, the maximum limit of expenditure of the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management resulting from the entry into force of this law in 2018 will be 3150 thousand. zł. This amendment was accepted by the government and voted by the committee. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The law should enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, with the exception of Article. 1 concerning tasks carried out by the National Reference and Modeling Laboratory, which should enter into force on the day following the date of publication. I will mention that the national laboratory is ready to undertake and carry out these tasks immediately. Finally, I would like to stress once again that the amendment of the Environmental Inspection Act and the Environmental Protection Law is necessary for Poland to fulfil its obligations as a Member State of the European Union to transpose the provisions of the Directive laying down provisions on reference methods, data approval and location of measuring points for air quality assessment. On behalf of the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry, I recommend that the High House adopt a draft government act amending the Environmental Inspection Act and the Environmental Protection Law. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u293	Thank you very much, sir. And I'd like to welcome the students from the Salesian schools of Szczecin to the gallery. Welcome to the Seym. ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u294	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! This draft law on the amendment of the Environmental Law will introduce, following the entry into force of the Act, provisions to improve the quality of atmospheric air tests, as well as the strengthening of the system for assessing the environmental and human health effects of pollutants. The new rules will also allow citizens free and universal access to information on the distribution of air pollution concentrations across all voivodships, as well as air quality forecasts. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! In the exposé delivered yesterday, Prime Minister Morawiecki spoke about the need to provide clean air for Poles, about energy poverty, which must be combated, with 40% lower electricity tariffs in hours outside the peak of energy demand, about the government's clean air programme. This shows the great importance that the Government of Law and Justice attaches to solving this problem. These actions will be supported by the implementation of new European legislation. The Act in print No 2066 amends to Polish law the provisions of Directive 2015/1480 of 28 August 2015, which amends certain annexes to Directives 2004/107/EC and 2008/50/EC laying down provisions on reference methods, data approval and location of measurement points for air quality assessment. Amendments are made to the Environmental Inspection Act - these are the amendments contained in Article. 1 - and in the Environmental Law - amendments are contained in Article. 2. The rules introduced extend and clarify the tasks of the National Reference and Standard Laboratory, which will allow the Voivodship's survey environment inspectors to carry out the proper quality - atmospheric air. Extended tasks of the National Reference and Modeling Laboratory include: reviewing quality assurance and control systems in institutions operating networks and individual measuring points at least every 5 years; coordinating the proper use of reference methodologies and demonstrating equivalence of non-reference methodologies; participating at least once every 3 years in quality assurance programmes prepared by the European Commission; coordinating quality assurance programmes prepared by the European Commission in Poland and supporting the work carried out by the European Commission's Joint Research Centre. High Izbo! So far, there has been no uniform system solution in Poland regarding the principles of modelling of mathematical transport and transformation of substances in the air, at national level, supporting the assessment of air quality at voivodship level. It should be stressed that the annual assessment of air quality in zones - in case of class C qualification, i.e. where the concentration level exceeds the limit or target level - is the basis for the preparation by the voivodship board of a draft air protection programme which is adopted by means of a local act, i.e. by a resolution of the Sejm of voivodship. The proposed amendment to the Environmental Law will provide the air quality assessment bodies, i.e. the General Environmental Inspector and the Regional Environmental Inspectors, with a uniform method of supporting air quality assessments in zones using mathematical modelling. This task will be carried out by the Institute for Environmental Protection - the State Research Institute. This unit has extensive experience in air quality assessments, including using mathematical modelling methods. As a result of the entry into force of the legal solutions discussed here, it will ensure that mathematical transport and the transformation of substances into air are carried out at national level, i.e. modelling consistent methodologically and based on reliable and up-to-date emission data collected systematically within the framework of KOBIZ, i.e. the National Centre for Balancing and Emissions Management, because this is the role played by the Institute for Environmental Protection - the State Research Institute. The results of modelling carried out by the same body with a uniform methodology will be comparable, consistent and complete and available to each Environmental Inspection Unit. Thus, a coherent system will be made available to the public at national level for forecasting exceedances of limit and alert levels. This single, coherent system will ensure comparability of the results obtained for the following years, will allow the examination of trends in air quality, will allow the assessment of cross-border transmission of pollutants, particularly from the Czech Republic and Germany, and will enable the Environment Minister to assess the effectiveness of the air protection measures taken. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! [[♪ The bell ♪]] The Law and Justice Club will vote in favour of the bill. Thank you very much. We then reported the need to raise awareness among citizens of the principles of safe arms handling as a result of a long-term educational process prior to possible liberalisation of access to arms. I would point out here that our position on the liberalisation of access to arms remains unchanged and we will not support a project which, with the current state of public awareness and lack of arms, would increase access to it. But this project is different. Its objectives focus mainly on education and the creation of an environment conducive to the development of pro-defence organisations. The project envisages the introduction of a concept of school weapons which will not allow the shooting to be fired while at the same time giving the opportunity to familiarize themselves with the construction and mechanisms of operation of the weapon, which will enable safe learning to use it. As opposed to non-commercial weapons, school weapons are intended to have movable elements, which will increase the quality and efficiency of training. Another change allows the use of ammunition with full-coat shells containing core made of harder material than lead alloy, among others. by associations directly related to defence or State security or the protection of public policy, by schools which have concluded an agreement with the Ministry of National Defence or the Minister of Home Affairs on the implementation of defence training as well as by shooters. This change, motivated by the desire to pass on the above-mentioned amendment. arms and ammunition operators, which are unnecessary to the Armed Forces, have a justification not only for economic reasons, because we will avoid costly disposal but also pro-educational ones. A logical approach is to develop unnecessary arms and ammunition in the army and to provide training to military personnel with training for military service is a common sense solution that will positively affect the level of ability of recruits to use weapons in the future. However, consideration should be given to regulating the further disposal of the transferred weapons and ammunition so that it does not become a source of income for privileged organisations that may charge for making it available during training. Following the justification, the transfer I understand as a form of free provision, and therefore the use of these weapons and ammunition should also be free of charge in order to make defence education more popular. The creation of a central and accessible set of examination questions for applicants for arms permits will in turn positively contribute to equalising the chances of these candidates. These questions can be e.g. 200 or 300. This change will eliminate the possibility of deliberately choosing easier or more difficult questions for the applicant to facilitate or hinder his/her view of the theoretical examination. A unified set of questions is consequently intended to equalise the chances of all candidates and ensure that the knowledge of those who passed the test is sufficiently high. The last change to allow the use of noise silencers, although only on shooting range, is also a positive change. In my view, the project should be assessed positively. Amenities are introduced to increase the level of education in arms use. The changes also offer the possibility of using weapons and ammunition withdrawn from the military, which is additionally economic. To sum up, as I said at the outset, I personally take the project as a kind of response to the modern need to increase awareness among citizens of the principles of safe operation of weapons in contrast to the extremely irresponsible liberalisation of access to arms. This project seems to be a common sense approach to the education of citizens in arms management. I hope that this educational process will be long-term, because only in this way can it be effective. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u392	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the proposal for a parliamentary bill amending the law on arms and ammunition. The suspension of compulsory forms of active military service and training of military graduates of higher education has resulted in a drastic reduction of the supply of trained soldiers to the stock of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. In response to the above. the circumstances of the Ministry of National Defence have taken action to create a system of voluntary training of the Armed Forces reserves using uniform classes, training of students within the Academic League, as well as pro-defence organisations. In order for the military training process to be effective and to respond to the demands of the Armed Forces, it is planned to transfer already unnecessary military equipment for training purposes. I consider this solution to be most appropriate, as this will significantly contribute to reducing the costs borne by the State Treasury for the disposal of ammunition, which ends in validity, and to the efficient use of withdrawn military equipment. In order to implement these objectives, it is necessary to make minor changes to the current arms access regulation system. The amendment of the law introduces among others. a new type of weapons - training weapons, allows the use of a noise silencer during shooting at the shooting range, extends the catalogue of ponies entitled to own self-arm with the shooters' operators and pro-defence organisations which have an agreement with the MON or the Minister responsible for Home Affairs on the implementation of defence training. There is no doubt that it is necessary to rebuild the infrastructure for military training. Shooting range, which is close to the place of residence, has often encountered considerable difficulties in functioning. Therefore, action taken to reduce the negative impact of shooters on the surrounding areas seems to be a very important and desirable element for communities living nearby. Such a solution is to be able to use a bang silencer when shooting shots. This solution will certainly help to reduce the negative impact of shooters on the environment. Applicants shall take the relevant examination. The proposed act introduces an obligation for the Chief of Police and the Polish Association of Sports Shooting Central Register of Examination Questions to be identified and made available by the Chief of Police. This solution aims to ensure the correct level of knowledge and to harmonise the knowledge needed to pass the exam across the country. I consider the amendment of the law to be justified in this respect. I think that the solutions put in place will have a positive impact on the growth of the interest in shooting, will contribute to the creation of a system of voluntary training of Polish Armed Forces personnel reserves and will affect the development of small infrastructure. Personally, I think that access to weapons should be much easier for citizens. However, I understand that not everything is possible immediately. About Free and Solidarni supports the bill submitted.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u394	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The resolution of the Sejm of the Republic of Poland dated - and here it will be shown which - 2017 on the celebration of the Polish avant-garde on the 100th anniversary of its inauguration. In 2017, 100 years have passed since the start of the Polish avant-garde - an artistic movement, which radically changed the way of thinking about art, setting its new directions and goals. The official beginning of the Polish avant-garde was recognized as a manifesto of the opening of the First Expressionist Exhibition of Poland on 4 November 1917 at the headquarters of the Association of Friends of Fine Arts in Cracow, where the canon of works by Leon Chwistk, Titus Czyżewski and Zbigniew Pronaszki was presented today. Through their artistic activities, Polish avant-gardes significantly influenced the process of social change which took place in Poland in the beginning of the 20th century. The creativity of artists such as Stanisław Ignacy Witkiewicz, Władysław Strzemiński, Katarzyna Kobro, Tadeusz Peiper and representatives of corresponding and discussing movements with their activities - futurists and chamanders - was a driving force for the development of open culture and at the same time influenced the formation of the artistic identity of independent Poland. Polish avant-garde as an innovative artistic direction characterized by the search for new forms has significantly influenced the development of visual arts, theatre, film and architecture, emphasising the Polish position on the cultural map of the world. Her traditions continued for generations of plasticists, including Roman Opalka, Henryk Stażewski, Wojciech Fangor, Alina Szapocznikow, Natalia LL, Tadeusz Kantor. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland, appreciating the special role and importance of the avant-garde movement in creating the interwar culture of Poland, commemorates the 100th anniversary of its inauguration. Referring to yesterday's exposé by Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki, who said that culture is important, and that was the only thing he said about Polish culture actually, I want to make it clear that Polish culture is an area of freedom. Thinking about the Polish avant-garde and about the 100th anniversary of a very, very important time for the Polish avant-garde, I want to recall two institutions. Here I am thinking about the Museum of Art in Łódź, which is an absolute museum event in the history of Poland. It is an extremely open, modern museum which gathered in its collections works of best artists and best artists. And I think it is worth recalling three names here: Marian Monk, Ryszard Stanisławski - a long-standing partner of the outstanding Polish sculptor, who made a world career - after death, but despite all that was the crowning of her work, I am thinking about Alina Szapoczników, at the Museum of Modern Art in New York a few months ago there was a monographic exhibition of this outstanding artist, and Jan Suchan, who initiated the celebration of the 100th anniversary of the Polish avant-garde. The second person of the institution is Bożena Kowalska - art critic, art historian, a person who for many years, actually always accompanied the Polish vanguard. Between 1972 and 2001, she ran Galeria 72 in Chelm. It was a very important, prestigious gallery. There used to be such a time that in the Polish province, in small cities it was possible to run really outstanding, extremely ambitious galleries, which presented the best works of art. ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u398	Mrs. Marshal! More than 50 institutions have already joined the cooperation. The celebrations are held under the honorary patronage of the President of the Republic of Poland Andrzej Duda and under the auspices of the Polish Committee for Foreign Affairs. UNESCO. The opening of the First Exhibition of Polish Expressionists on 4 November 1917 at the headquarters of the Kraków Association of Friends of Fine Arts can be considered a symbolic beginning of the Polish avant-garde. The Avant-garde had a pan-European reach and had a decisive impact on the development of modern aesthetic thought and artistic practice around the world. Its creators presented a new look at the form and function of art, and its distinctive features - openness to experimentation and continuous search for new forms - directly influenced cultural activities of later generations. At that time Poland was an important centre of this movement, and native artists brought important original quality to it. The works of Władysław Strzemiński, Katarzyna Kobro, Stanisław Ignacy Witkiewicz, Tadeusz Peiper and Szymon Syrkus represented an important voice in the international discussion on modernity. Due to their achievements Poland has become an important point on the map of world culture. Despite the huge influence on culture, among others. urban planning and architecture, industrial design and fashion, knowledge of Polish avant-garde in general awareness is still small, therefore it is worth commemorating the anniversary of its 100th anniversary. I would also like to remind on this occasion the poet Jan Brzękowski, who acted in the circle of the Avant-garde of Krakow. After his departure to France, he was an editor of the magazine "L'Art Contemporain', and he made close contacts with French artists. Friendly with Fernand Léger and Hans Arp, like Władysław Strzemiński, he gathered a large collection of works by French avant-garde painters, which they then handed to the city of Łódź. It was these collections that became the beginning of the magnificent collections of contemporary art museum in Łódź founded in 1930. The Civic Platform Club supports this resolution. [[Applause]]

Ossolinski was located in the former monastery and the church of Carmelite refurbished, among others. Józef Bem and Jerzy's efforts. Ossolineum focused Polish mental movement and was one of the most important centres of work on building Polish culture during partitions and imposed Germanization. During that time, the persecution period was in the form of police searches and arrests of workers employed there. In the intention of the founder it became one of the most important centres of research on Polish history and literature, especially since it disposes of one of the largest collections in Poland, as well as a huge collection of manuscripts and autographs, including medieval manuscripts and oldest prints. Further fates of Ossolineum reflected the difficult and tragic fate of Poland, which became its participation in the 20th century. After the Soviet Union seized Lviv in September 1939. The Ossolineum has undergone a major reorganisation. In 1940, the legal status of the plant was abolished by Soviet occupiers, the collections were nationalised and the plant itself became a Soviet order absorbed by the Academy of Sciences of the Soviet Union and became its subsidiary. During the first Soviet occupation Lviv was requisitioned in Ossolineum, among others. Polish deposits of gold and silver placed here by the Polish aristocracy and landownership. During the German occupation of Lviv, the library was incorporated into the structure of the German Staatbibliothek Lemberg and in 1944 the German authorities exported its library collections. The war successfully survived in Lower Silesia and in 1947 reactivated the Ossolineum Library in Wroclaw. In 1953, Narodowy Zakład im. Ossolinski was incorporated into the structure of the Polish Academy of Sciences and divided into two separate institutions. In 1995 the Sejm of the Republic of Poland re-established the Foundation of the National Plant. Ossolinski. This year, on the 200th anniversary of the functioning of the Ossolineum, the Polish Parliament wants to honor this great national institution with an appropriate resolution. The Kukiz Club15 strongly supports this initiative. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u426	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a bill amending the National Court Register Act and some other laws. The project I am presenting represents another stage in the reform of economic law introduced by the Ministry of Justice. After the changes in the Commercial Companies Code and the creation of new restructuring law solutions, it was time for fundamental reforms concerning the National Court Register. The solutions envisaged in the draft are intended to implement the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 14 June 2017 on certain aspects of company law, and this Directive has been codified in the company law directives, including the Directive of the Parliament and of the Council of 13 June 2012 amending Council Directive 89/666 and the Directive of the Parliament and of the Council as regards the integration of central registers, commercial registers and company registers, i.e. so-called. the BRIS Directive. The deadline for implementation expired on 8 June 2017. The proposed amendments, on the one hand, aim at making it easier for the parties to start their business, also in communication with the court, and on the other, will significantly speed up and improve the functioning of the registry court, including the integration of central registers, commercial registers and company registers. The proposed reform of the National Court Register covers a wide range of technical changes which will fundamentally change the functioning of the National Court Register, and the most important of which is, first, the introduction of an obligation to submit financial statements in electronic form. The electronic form of the financial statements will enhance the possibilities for the development and analysis of the data contained in these documents. Such automated verification of financial statements will provide a significant relief for registration courts during reporting periods, i.e. from June to September each year. The financial statements will be included in the repository available on the website of the Ministry of Justice. Secondly, it is the creation of a publicly available electronic file of an entity which will cover all documents submitted to those files by the parties and documents produced by the court, as well as the increasing use of the possibility of electronic service of judicial documents and the introduction of the possibility of tracking proceedings on an ongoing basis, which also entails the possibility of receiving information about entries in the register, so-called. newsletter. Finally, after a number of facilitations have been introduced for the parties, we plan to introduce an obligation for entities subject to registration to apply to a registered court only in electronic form, which will significantly facilitate communication between the party and the court and speed up the recognition of applications. Full data will also be made available on the Internet about the entities included in the KRS, which means that in addition to the current copy that is already available on the network, you will also be able to obtain a full copy from the register by this way. The proposed solutions will be introduced in stages, depending on whether or not they require, and, if so, to the extent possible, changes to the electronic system of the National Court Register. In addition, the draft shall propose the verification of criminality to the extent set out in Article. 18 § 2 Commercial Companies Code of persons appointed as procurators. This will enhance the safety of economic turnover. Currently, persons who cannot act as members of the Management Board because of their prior criminality shall be appointed as procurators and shall in principle represent the registry entity without restrictions. Further, we propose the liquidation of the Register of Insolvent Debtors, which will relieve the registration courts and allow faster recognition of applications for entry in the Register. And the last of the changes is to extend the powers of the curators to eliminate pathological situations when companies cancel the boards only so that it is not possible to conduct legal proceedings against them. It seems that the project is not controversial, and it allows, I think, to speed up court proceedings, to introduce some modern solutions into our register law, and therefore I ask for its support. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u428	Yes, thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I happen to be running a committee meeting, and I thought it was a little longer for the Minister... but I made it. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the draft law amending the Act on the National Court Register, print No 2067. The project concerns the introduction of facilitations for the parties at the start of business and accelerates and improves the functioning of the registry court and provides for facilitation in the contact of the parties with the court. Clearly, the proposed regulation is another step in the economic reform of the National Court Register. The main changes contained in the government project aim, first, to strengthen the certainty and safety of trading. Secondly, they relate to the use of new information technologies and take into account the necessary Directive 2017/1132/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council on the integration of central, commercial and company registers into implementation. Thirdly, they aim at simplifying the so-called. registration entries in the register of traders and a change in the manner and extent of disclosure of the arrears of entities. Fourthly, it also includes the submission of procedural and order changes. With regard to the strengthening of trading certainty, the project contains a number of solutions relating primarily to the representation of the economic operator, because that is the main problem at the moment, so there are regulations in it that resolve certain doubts and give, as it were, opportunities in this area. In addition, it is proposed, as regards new technologies, to require all applications to be submitted to the Register of Entrepreneurs using the electronic system, to create a Central Repository for Electronic Records of Notarial Acts, to introduce an obligation to submit financial statements in electronic form in an appropriate data format, to integrate the National Court Register with the system for the integration of registers referred to in the Directive of the European Parliament, to make available on the Internet full data on the subject and to automate certain entries, to simplify the making of so-called. the registration entries and a change in the scope of disclosure of the entity's arrears. The minimum amount of arrears to be recorded shall be determined while allowing one entry to be made on the basis of several implementing titles. It is proposed to disclose in the Central Register of Restructuring and Failures information on the arrears of entities and not to include in the Register of insolvent debtors and maintenance debtors. In other words, all these changes are going in such a way that indeed the National Court Register will meet the challenges that are also linked to economic development and an increase in the number of economic operators. Of course, the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice will support these changes. On behalf of the club, I request that the project be directed to further legislative work. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u430	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, I would like to present a position regarding a government draft law amending the National Court Register Act and some other laws, print No 2067. The project concerns changes in the National Court Register, which include changes in the National Court Register. on the obligation for traders to submit applications to the National Court Register in electronic form, financial statements in electronic form and to keep records in electronic form. It is appropriate to agree with the statement in the explanatory memorandum to the draft law, which we say, that within 16 years of the functioning of the National Court Register, it has become an important source of information about entities, strengthening the certainty and safety of trading. In addition to the discussion, the overall number of entities remaining in the jurisdiction of the registry court is increasing from year to year. The figures show this. For example, in 2010 more than 23 000 were entered in the register of entrepreneurs. entities, and in 2015 more than 48 thousand were already registered. entities. The same is true of the register of associations, other social and professional organisations, foundations and independent healthcare establishments. This also applies to the debtor register. It is worth noting that the bill was subject to extensive public consultation. Nearly 30 stakeholders were involved: organisations bringing together lawyers, employers, auditors, trade union organisations, the Financial Supervision Commission, the Supreme Court, the Supreme Administrative Court, appeals courts and others. This consultation resulted in a rather broad report, as the actors I was just talking about presented a number of comments. Some of them have been taken into account. I would like to draw attention to some of them, mainly those rejected, because I believe that they should be taken into account in further work on the draft in committee. The National Chamber Council has proposed that the qualifications of persons who can act as guardian of the legal person be legally defined. The National Council of Legal Counsels has proposed, among a number of comments, to extend the conditions for the appointment of a curator also in cases where the entity has no other than the management of the authorities, resulting in a legal person's inability to act. The Conference of Financial Enterprises in Poland and the Confederation of Leviathan requested that, on an equal basis by means of teletransmission of data, access to the Central Register of Restructuring and Impairment, as well as broadening the scope of the disclosures, be granted free of charge to all economic information offices. The Financial Supervision Commission has proposed to expand the subject matter of the project e.g. the obligation to notify the KNF of the opening of the procedure for the appointment of a curator and to consult the Financial Supervision Committee on the person to be established as a curator. Very important comments, in my opinion, have been made by the General Data Protection Officer, who has argued that making records available through the ICT system will mean significant interference in the privacy of citizens. The data contained in the register will not only become public but also widely available, which will have far-reaching legal effects. I think that such opinions should also be thoroughly analysed in the further development of the project we are talking about. According to the draft law, the provisions enter into force on the day following the date of publication, with exceptions, as the provisions enter into force in parts: 1 February 2018, 1 October 2018, 1 February 2019 and 1 March 2020. It seems that this time for the preparation of the registry courts for this change is sufficient, but this aspect should also be examined so that the system operates smoothly and does not cause any confusion in the legal trade. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club, I would like to say that we are in favour of directing this project to further parliamentary work. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u442	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I'll answer the questions in order to ask them. These are the work we are debating today, because certainly the entry into force of this law, if the High House and the Higher House, and then the President... If all this is properly processed, there is a chance that the efficiency of these registration procedures will improve. We work not only from the legislative side, but also from the organisational side. It was during these governments that were assigned - at this moment I do not have accurate statistics - many new references to the departments of the National Court Register, especially to these three departments of Warsaw, which are actually besieged, because thousands, tens of thousands of applications are involved. We assign referenda there, we assign service staff there, without which the court, especially the registry court, will not function. This is, of course, not sufficient because we are dealing with restrictions on the IT system. It's just that the IT system was built or designed over 20 years ago. He was out there, a little bit improved up-to-date, but this is a system from another era. This is a system that already constitutes a dam in terms of the smooth functioning of the judicial register, and this requires extensive analytical work - which is being carried out - and then technical work, so that this system is a 21st century system. We have signals, we monitor these signals, especially from the Warsaw court, that the referenda are ready, and the court staff are ready. The system doesn't allow for faster work. The hours of work are organized by the management of the registry court so that there are people who work earlier before the normal hours of the court's work, and those who work after hours of work. Elastic work is organised to exploit the capabilities of the IT system, because trying to make too many entries at the same time causes the system to fail, possibly the system analyzes the data so long that it is not possible to carry out further actions efficiently. And this is an IT system, you can't take another card here and write or print it. It all has to work in the system. So it can be said that the many years of neglect of our predecessors have led to the fact that we have a very inefficient IT system and we have to deal with it. We are directing new forces to these courts and we will improve the functioning of the IT system, which should significantly improve the situation, but as you know, large IT projects are not only expensive but also long-term, so from day to day the effects will not be, but we are on the right track. The deadline of 3 days for making the file available is indeed due to the size of the Warsaw KRS - because that is where it works - because of the need to rotate tens of thousands of registered files. I can answer in this way: if this is possible, of course this deadline will be shortened, but we will definitely end the problems that you mention when this law comes into force and the system will be improved, adapted to it. Then there will be no time limit for waiting to make the records available, because they will be available at any time from anywhere in the world. Now these records have a physical form, you have to date to see them. This is so many documents that the courts, especially those located on the street. Czerniakowska in Warsaw is simply not able to share these files in a shorter period of time. The electronicisation of this process will actually eliminate the problem. Ms Kołacz-Leszczyńska from the Platform asked about the scope of the curator's competence, why we are eliminating this project of the registry curator while expanding the powers of the curator with the article. 42 Civil Code. This is mentioned in the justification of the project, but let me remind you: the point is that the registry curator did not work out, it was generally not used. We have recorded dozens of cases over 16 years of operation of the National Court Register in this form. Possibility to establish so-called The registry curator was limited only to the register of entrepreneurs of the National Court Register, and yet the register of entrepreneurs, however largest, is not the only one, because we have a register of foundations, associations, SPOZs, etc., etc. Other registered entities could not benefit from the existence of a registered curator institution. The extension of the scope of the institutions of the curator from the Civil Code to all entities that are subject to a registered obligation in any civil proceedings completely settles the issue. Therefore, it does not seem appropriate to maintain a narrower and poorly used institution of the probation officer. In her question, Mrs Czernow raised the question of the length of the procedure to be deleted from the register. The procedure for deletion from the register is the same as any other case, and because of its subject matter, this procedure sometimes takes a long time. However, I would like to draw attention to the possibility of the so-called existing law. resolution without liquidation, Article. 25a and subsequent provisions of the National Court Register Act. The application of this solution greatly accelerates the handling of those whom you mention, i.e. those who de facto have no assets, have not started their activities, in fact their existence is merely a formality, and it is simply necessary to carry out appropriate acts of diligence in order to eliminate such entities. So here this solution without liquidation is a good alternative. However, if we have signals that any proceedings take longer than necessary, the Ministry of Justice will, of course, be interested in the administrative supervision of the activities of universal courts, including registered courts, in such a case, but this is already in a specific case. As regards the issue raised by Mr Meysztowicz from the Modern, i.e. the question of resignation from membership in the governing body of a legal person, it is a declaration of will which results from the mere fact that he has submitted it, and therefore there is no constitutionality to the entry on the deletion of someone from the judicial register, that he, for example. shall cease to be a member of the Management Board as a result of the resignation. It is, of course, important that this entry be made as soon as possible, because on the other hand we have the presumption that what is disclosed in the register is consistent with the actual state of affairs, but as I mentioned, the nature of the alert here is purely declaratory, and it has often been used by dishonest entities, making such waivers for the purpose of paralyzing legal proceedings. The project also deals with this phenomenon through the possibility of setting up a probation officer. As a result, the process in which an entity is party, where all persons from the management body are subject to such mass resignation, may take place. As regards the issue of preparation for the implementation of these changes, they are repeated, on several occasions, declarations by notaries who are obliged to prepare the CREWAN system, that they will make it in time, that there are no problems. So we have no reason to question these statements. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u444	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party our position on the government draft law amending the Act on the National Court Register and some other laws (print No 2067). Today's governmental draft amendment to the Act on the National Court Register and some other laws assumes that from 1 March 2020 entrepreneurs will be required to submit applications to the National Court Register in electronic form. Associations, foundations and public health establishments will have a choice as to whether to use electronic form. The aim of this solution is to improve the functioning of the National Court Register. Also in electronic form, requests will be sent to remedy the deficiencies, letters submitted and remedies. Since 1 March 2020, registration files are to be kept in electronic form, but financial documents will be submitted in electronic form before that date. Entrepreneurs will be required to submit financial statements electronically. The financial statements will be transmitted by the National Court Register to the Ministry of Finance. The application for entry of persons representing the trader and the winding-up and procurators will need to be accompanied, in addition to the declaration of appointment, by addresses. Judgments terminating proceedings shall be issued and served in electronic form. It will not be necessary to provide a justification for a provision in matters other than the alert if the applicant and the participants have taken part in the proceedings. If an application is submitted in electronic form, further letters will also have to be submitted in electronic form. The project envisages the creation of a Central Repository for Electronic Releases of Notarial Acts. According to the project, the institution of the registry curator shall be liquidated. The existing provisions will apply to proceedings before a registered court initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of this amendment. High Izbo, there is no doubt that today there is a need to inform and modernise the functioning of the administration, the judiciary and institutions such as the National Court Register. However, in agreement with the general objectives of the project, it should be asked whether it is necessary to remove the possibility for entrepreneurs to submit applications to the National Courts on a traditional, paper basis. Why shouldn't companies, like associations, foundations and public health establishments, be left to choose? Perhaps the transitional period for companies should be extended beyond the planned date of 1 March 2020. The government project, although worthwhile, requires further work in this area. Ladies and gentlemen, on behalf of the club I request that the project be directed to further work in committees.

The possibility of immediate termination of the contract with the tenant of the wheel is also introduced, as well as at the request of the Polish Łowiecki Union, if the tenant does not want to fulfil the obligations assumed and defined by the Government's Crisis Management Team. Veterinary Inspections are also authorised to make greater use of private practitioners who are not employees of the Veterinary Inspection by setting up teams. Epizootic investigations to relieve the burden on the district veterinarian in determining the source of the disease. It was said that, while the Polish-Hungarian friendship is very strong and durable, the lack of institutional, organisational and daily forms of cooperation is noted. Therefore, it was said that the resolution and subsequent legislative work were to lead to the establishment of the Foundation. Wenceslas Felczak, who is to take care of e.g. activities in the field of social, scientific, formation, economic and information, and of course in the field of culture, sport and education. It was also important that already in November this year similar actions were taken by the National Assembly, the Hungarian Parliament. As far as we know, probably already in March 2018 such a foundation, the Hungarian Foundation im. Wenceslas Felczak will begin his activities. To more concreteize the formula of bringing about the foundation's establishment. Wenceslas Felczak, a number of stylistic amendments were made in the draft resolution, mainly in the last paragraph, which after the change reads: The Polish Parliament asks the Government of the Republic of Poland to take a legislative initiative in order to establish the Foundation. Wenceslas Felczak. There were some disagreements in the discussion, some differences of opinion, but the Committee on Culture and the Centres of Transmission unanimously gave a positive opinion on this draft resolution. The Committee on Culture and Media therefore requests the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft resolution. During the discussion, however, one thread was very strongly pierced - that it would be very important and necessary for the work on this resolution, which is to result in the act and finally the establishment of the Foundation for them. Wenceslas Felczak, remind the character of this outstanding Pole, extremely well deserved for fighting for independence of Poland, but also for cooperation and Polish-Hungarian friendship. Prof. Dr. Hab. Wacław Felczak was born on 29 May 1916 in the village of Golbice in the district of Lęczycki, and died on 23 October 1993 in Krakow. He was a Polish historian, an expert on Central European issues, especially Hungarian and South Slavic. Already in his youth he became involved in political activity and connected with the Christian-democratic trend. After his history studies, he applied for a scholarship to go to Hungary. There he conducted scientific research, primarily source research, archival research. However, in the summer of 1939 he returned to Poland and after the September campaign he connected himself with the independence underground, the Armed Fighting Union, the later National Army, and began his brave courier service. Between 1940 and 1945 he was a courier of the government of the Republic of Poland in exile, still on the way between London and the country. Here the important thing is that when he traveled this route many times, Budapest was an important place on this route. Here, in fact, he has created a suitable facility, which has become one of the most important stages of this courier route. In these difficult times of occupation, he made many friends in Hungary. They became, and even more so, the history of this country, not only the subject of its scientific interests, but also, you can say, almost the second homeland of the later Prof. Wenceslas Felczak. After World War II he continued his activity for independence of Poland, this time against communist, Soviet invaders. Until 1948, he continued his courier activity, of course, by contacting the government of the Republic of Poland on exile, the only legal Polish government. In 1948, he was arrested by Communists and sentenced to life imprisonment after a long investigation. He was only released from prison after eight years, in October 1956. Since 1958, he has been an employee of the Jagiellonian University Institute of History, PhD in 1962 under the direction of Professor Henryk Vereszycki. Here it is worth to add that Prof. Henryk Vereszycki is also a man with rich achievements in the field of independence activity, a soldier of Polish legions, a participant of the Polish-Bolshevik war. Professor... not yet professor, later professor Wacław Felczak received habilitation in 1968. Since the second half of the 1970s, he has systematically travelled to Hungary and played a significant role in the formation of a democratic, independent opposition which led to the collapse of Communists in Hungary in 1989. It can be said that it even inspired the Hungarian opposition environments to a more concrete form of independence, anti-communist activity - among others. from those environments that inspired anti-communist activity, the reigning Hungarians of Fidesz grew up today. What is very important, the communist authorities of the Polish People's Republic in 1975 blocked the request of the Jagiellonian University to appoint Wacław Felczak as ordinary professor. Only after 18 years, in the independent Republic of Poland, on 5 October 1993 he became a professor. He was already in the hospital, terminally ill, died after a dozen days. So it can be said that throughout the communist rule Professor Wacław Felczak was severely persecuted by Communists. It is also worth adding that Prof. Wacław Felczak was the author, among others. the fundamental history of Hungary. I end up thinking that regardless of the main purpose of setting up the Foundation for them. Wenceslas Felczak and the stronger institutional development of Polish-Hungarian cooperation is also very important to commemorate, reminding this great Pole, Professor Wenceslas Felczak. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

It lasted from morning hours on 13 December to 3 p.m. 2 of 15 December 1981 Several thousand people took part in it. At the end of the strike, about 1750 steel mills - 1500 in the Metallurgical Works and 250 in the Steel and Processing Works - left the bets. The Hutniks established the Strike Committee, which subsequently became the Union Savings Committee, set up the workers' guards, announced the demands for the release of interned colleagues. This is another step in the right direction, towards taking care of the most important thing, about the possibility of spending time together, among the closest ones. I am convinced that the newly appointed government under the direction of Mr Mateusz Morawicki will continue the policy of sustainable development of the country, with particular emphasis on the safety of the Polish family. To conclude, I would like to wish all the inhabitants of Starogard and the district of Starogard for the coming Christmas. Many health, family warmth and blessings of God's Child. ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u575	Thank you very much. The statement will now be made by Małgorzata Chmiel. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u576	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! As a Member of Pomorskie Land, I tabled six amendments to the 2018 budget concerning money for investments in Pomorskie voivodship. The first amendment concerns the allocation of additional funds to the second stage of the renovation of the historic bridge on the Vistula Vistula in Tczew. A few weeks ago, local government officials from Tczewski County announced an invitation to tender for the renovation of this bridge. After opening the offers, it turned out that the cheapest offer exceeded the amount they have, by about PLN 30 million. I therefore call on Members to support my amendment increasing funding for the renovation of the Vistula bridge in Tczew. My second amendment concerns the granting of money to carry out design work related to the Tricity Metropolitan Circuit. The current Tricity ring road has not met any applicable traffic standards for several years. During peak hours, there are large traffic jams almost daily, which make it not an alternative for drivers who want to get around Gdańsk, Gdynia and Sopot. My next amendments concern the extension of nodes on the Tricity ring road - the Kowale node and Shadółka node. At present, they do not meet the standards applied to nodes on high-speed roads. Their expansion will significantly improve safety and significantly reduce traffic congestion both on the ring road and on the access roads to it. The fifth amendment I tabled concerns the financing of the project and the construction of the Gdansk Starogard ring road. At present, the national road no.22, which runs through the centre of Starogard, is blocked during peak hours, causing nuisance for residents, as well as situations that threaten their health and life. My last amendment concerns the allocation of funds for documentation of the construction of the Green Road along with the tunnel near Pachołek in Gdańsk. This road would be the first direct route to the Tricity ring road from Sopot. Its construction will also allow you to bypass the historic post-Cistercian Olive, through which many thousands of cars pass every day, causing irreparable damage to the historic Olive Park, as well as to the post-Cistercian Oliwska Archicathedral. Unfortunately, all these amendments have already been rejected at the meeting of the finance committee by the PiS Members, including the PiS Members from our district, so that I am very, very sorry. Nevertheless, I will continue to defend them in the plenary session of the Sejm. I therefore ask Members, especially those from Pomerania, to support these important investments for our region. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u577	Thank you. Mrs Małgorzata Zwierzan, near Free and Solidarni.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-12-13-sejm-54-2.u578	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 13 December 1981, martial law was introduced. It was one of the darkest days in the latest history of Poland. The official reasons for introducing martial law were the country's bad situation and the threat of energy security. But it was just a game of appearances. In fact, the communists were afraid of losing power because the decline in social trust in the authorities of the Polish People's Republic was very large. It was feared that normal Poles who learned the taste of democracy would claim power, creating an Independent Self-Governmental Trade Union.Solidarity. Poles were threatened by the aggression of foreign troops, and today we know that the Soviets involved in the conflict in Afghanistan were not only interested in intervention in Poland, but were not even agreed to introduce martial law with them. This was done by our - in quotes - popular power by the hands of one man, Soviet agent Wojciech Jaruzelski, who led to the tragedy of many Polish families. It didn't take long for real patriots to react. There have been further strikes, among others. in Gdańsk Shipyard im. Lenin, Hucie im. Lenina in Cracow, Truck Factory in Lublin, Shipyard im. Warskiego in Szczecin, in Huta Katowice, as well as in coal mines in Silesia. The ZOMO killed dozens of strikers, and even more rebels against power were injured. Extreme situation of martial law began mass conspiracy activities. Many Poles spontaneously manifested their resistance to Communists: they sold leaflets and posters, painted numerous anti-government inscriptions on the walls, and an underground publishing movement was created. There was no end to the repression for these actions. Violence was used, work was fired. Internment, arrests, court sentences are the everyday life of martial law, which changed many Poles: he forced some to emigrate, others to hide for a long time. There were those who were broken and still cannot find themselves in the normal world. The economic situation of many Polish families has also deteriorated. The communist authorities have wronged us, the Polish people, beyond repair. Therefore, I do not understand the defenders of General Jaruzelski, the opponents of changing the names of the streets or other objects worshiping criminals, the cameramen of the former system. Today we must remember those who at that time fought for the independent existence of the Polish state, about our heroes. Today we should remember those who, for our sovereignty, for their true freedom, were victims. First of all, I remind you of this because this story never happens again. The times when power was armed against citizens fighting in the right cause, when the next day was uncertain, when one Pole reported on another, they could not repeat themselves again. I am convinced that many of us attended the December celebrations today and honored those who died. Let this historical memory be an opportunity for us to reflect and, most importantly, contribute to national consent. Hello their memory! [[Applause]]
